## Q50

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

Adult human RBCs are enucleate. Which of the following statement(s) is/are most appropriate explanation for this feature?

- **(a)** They do not need to reproduce
- **(b)** They are somatic cells
- **(c)** They do not metabolize
- **(d)** All their internal space is available for oxygen transport

- (1) Only (a)
- (2) (a), (c) and (d)
- (3) (b) and (c)
- **(4)** Only (d)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In Human RBCs, nucleus degenerates during maturation which provide more space for oxygen carrying pigment (Haemoglobin).
2. It lacks most of the cell organelles including mitochondria so respires anaerobically.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement (a): not dividing is a consequence of lacking a nucleus, not the most appropriate explanation of why RBCs are enucleate.
2. Statement (b): most somatic cells are nucleated, so being somatic does not explain enucleation; (b) is not appropriate.
3. Statement (c): RBCs do metabolise (they rely on anaerobic glycolysis for ATP), so (c) is false.
4. Statement (d): without a nucleus the whole internal space can be filled with haemoglobin, maximising $O_2$ transport; (d) is the appropriate explanation.
5. Only (d) is appropriate, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Formed Elements And Their Types · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 10)

![printed question 50](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q050_e180c4f4_29102026.png?v=e180c4f4ce61)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 50](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q050_741ea9b5_29102026.png?v=741ea9b55ed9)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** A mature RBC gives up its nucleus so that the whole cell volume can be filled with haemoglobin; the gain is carrying capacity, not freedom from dividing
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 2  
  A student sure of (d) adds (c) from the remembered fact that the mature RBC has shed its mitochondria, reading 'no mitochondria' as 'no metabolism', and so takes the long combination instead of (d) alone.
- **Why:** This question requires the reason enucleation is useful to a mature RBC, demonstrated by the stem asking for the 'most appropriate explanation for this feature' and the key taking only (d), 'All their internal space is available for oxygen transport', while leaving out (b), which is true of the cell but explains nothing.
- **Note:** The record lists 'NOT' under negative_words_seen, but no negation is printed in this stem or in its options; the question is tagged as printed. Statement (a) is true of an enucleate cell, and the key rests on the stem's 'most appropriate', which selects (d).
<!--/ecat-->

## Q51

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

An important characteristic that Hemichordates share with Chordates is

- (1) Ventral tubular nerve cord
- **(2)** Pharynx with gill slits
- (3) Pharynx without gill slits
- (4) Absence of notochord

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Pharyngeal gill slits are present in hemichordates as well as in chordates.
2. Notochord is present in chordates only.
3. Ventral tubular nerve cord is characteristic feature of non-chordates.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Chordate hallmarks are a notochord, a dorsal hollow nerve cord and paired pharyngeal gill slits; of these, hemichordates have pharyngeal gill slits, so the shared characteristic is 'Pharynx with gill slits', option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Phylum – Echinodermata and Hemichordata · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q53

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

Identify the wrong statement in context of heartwood.

- (1) It is highly durable
- **(2)** It conducts water and minerals efficiently
- (3) It comprises dead elements with highly lignified walls
- (4) Organic compounds are deposited in it

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Heartwood is physiologically inactive due to deposition of organic compounds and tyloses formation, so this will not conduct water and minerals.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Heartwood (duramen) is the dark central secondary xylem made of dead elements with highly lignified walls, so option $3$ is correct.
2. Organic compounds such as tannins, resins, oils, gums and aromatic substances are deposited in heartwood, so option $4$ is correct.
3. These deposits make heartwood hard and highly durable, resistant to microorganisms and insects, so option $1$ is correct.
4. Heartwood does not conduct water; conduction of water and minerals is done by the peripheral sapwood (alburnum), so option $2$ is the wrong statement.

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 10)

![printed question 53](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q053_f46112e6_29102026.png?v=f46112e662e0)

<!--ecat-->

- **Concept:** Heartwood is the dead, lignified, deposit-filled core of the secondary xylem; the conducting is done by the sapwood around it.
- **Trap:** **Condition dropped** (`condition_missed`) — drop the condition the stem states and the option becomes true — option 3  
  Drop the stem's single word 'wrong' and option 3 becomes the model answer, because 'dead elements with highly lignified walls' is the line the chapter prints for heartwood.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated facts about heartwood graded one by one, demonstrated by the keyed option 'It conducts water and minerals efficiently', which is the sapwood's work.

## Q54

Biology · both · Biomolecules

Which one of the following statements is correct, with reference to enzymes?

- **(1)** Holoenzyme = Apoenzyme + Coenzyme
- (2) Coenzyme = Apoenzyme + Holoenzyme
- (3) Holoenzyme = Coenzyme + Cofactor
- (4) Apoenzyme = Holoenzyme + Coenzyme

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Holoenzyme is conjugated enzyme in which protein part is apoenzyme while non-protein is cofactor.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The protein portion of a conjugated enzyme is the $\text{apoenzyme}$, its non-protein organic part is the $\text{coenzyme}$, and together they form the active $\text{holoenzyme} = \text{apoenzyme} + \text{coenzyme}$, which is option $1$.

- **Concept:** A holoenzyme is the whole working enzyme: the protein apoenzyme together with its non-protein coenzyme.
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 4  
  The three words are held without which one names the whole, so the relation is written with a part on the left -- Apoenzyme = Holoenzyme + Coenzyme instead of Holoenzyme minus Coenzyme.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated definition of a holoenzyme, demonstrated by the four options printing the same three terms in different places against the keyed 'Holoenzyme = Apoenzyme + Coenzyme'.

## Q56

Biology · botany · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

Among the following characters, which one was not considered by Mendel in his experiments on pea?

- **(1)** Trichomes – Glandular or non-glandular
- (2) Seed – Green or Yellow
- (3) Pod – Inflated or Constricted
- (4) Stem – Tall or Dwarf

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. During his experiments Mendel studied seven characters.
2. Nature of trichomes i.e., glandular or non-glandular was not considered by Mendel.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Mendel studied seven pairs of contrasting traits in pea: stem height (tall/dwarf), flower colour (violet/white), flower position (axial/terminal), pod shape (inflated/constricted), pod colour (green/yellow), seed shape (round/wrinkled) and seed colour (yellow/green).
2. Options $2$ (seed colour), $3$ (pod shape) and $4$ (stem height) are all among these seven traits.
3. Trichomes (glandular or non-glandular) are not one of Mendel's seven traits, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Contrasting Traits Studied by Mendel in Pea · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 11)

## Q57

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

Which of the following facilitates opening of stomatal aperture?

- (1) Decrease in turgidity of guard cells
- **(2)** Radial orientation of cellulose microfibrils in the cell wall of guard cells
- (3) Longitudinal orientation of cellulose microfibrils in the cell wall of guard cells
- (4) Contraction of outer wall of guard cells

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Cellulose microfibrils are oriented radially rather than longitudinally which makes easy for the stoma to open.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Stomata open when guard cells become more turgid, so a decrease in turgidity (option $1$) closes them.
2. On turgor gain the thin outer walls bulge outward rather than contract, which rules out option $4$.
3. Cellulose microfibrils in guard cell walls are oriented radially rather than longitudinally, which makes it easier for the stoma to open, so option $2$ is correct and option $3$ is not.

**Subtopic:** Transpiration and Stomatal Opening and Closing · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 11)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 57](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q057_c57c9f43_29102026.png?v=c57c9f43e8b9)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated reason** (`reason_line`) — a reason NCERT gives: why it happens
- **Concept:** Stomata open when the guard cells gain turgor and their radially arranged cellulose microfibrils make the thin outer wall bow outwards, drawing the thick inner walls apart.
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 1  
  Turgidity is the mechanism the chapter gives for stomatal movement, and a student who has the mechanism but not its sense takes the turgidity option even though its direction is the one that closes the pore.
- **Why:** This question requires the reason the chapter gives for the aperture opening, demonstrated by the stem's 'facilitates opening of stomatal aperture' against the keyed 'Radial orientation of cellulose microfibrils in the cell wall of guard cells'.

## Q58

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

The association of histone H1 with a nucleosome indicates

- (1) DNA replication is occurring
- **(2)** The DNA is condensed into a Chromatin Fibre
- (3) The DNA double helix is exposed
- (4) Transcription is occurring

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The association of H1 protein indicates the complete formation of nucleosome.
2. Therefore the DNA is in condensed form.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Histone H1 is the linker histone: it binds the linker DNA where it enters and leaves the nucleosome core, which leads to further coiling of the beads-on-string into a condensed chromatin fibre, so the DNA is not exposed for replication or transcription; this is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Packaging of DNA Helix · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 11)

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Histone H1 is the linker histone that binds a nucleosome from outside and packs the beads-on-string into the condensed chromatin fibre.
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 4  
  Transcriptional activity is the fact printed alongside chromatin packing -- euchromatin loose and active, heterochromatin dense and inactive -- so a student holding the histone-and-transcription association reaches for it and attaches the active state to the wrong form.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact about what H1's association with a nucleosome signifies, demonstrated by the keyed 'The DNA is condensed into a Chromatin Fibre' set against three options naming activities. The swap test was applied to option 4: rename either term and the pull survives, because the transcription sentence's position beside the packing sentence is what carries it, so nearby_fact.

## Q59

Biology · both · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

DNA fragments are

- **(1)** Negatively charged
- (2) Neutral
- (3) Either positively or negatively charged depending on their size
- (4) Positively charged

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. DNA fragments are negatively charged because of phosphate group.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Each nucleotide in DNA has a phosphate group in the sugar-phosphate backbone, and each phosphate carries a negative charge ($\mathrm{PO_4^-}$), so any DNA fragment is negatively charged whatever its size. In gel electrophoresis the fragments therefore move towards the anode ($+$). This is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Separation & Isolation of DNA Fragments (Gel Electrophoresis) · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 11)

![printed question 59](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q059_88a85b98_29102026.png?v=88a85b98586e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 59](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q059_6bf25e4f_29102026.png?v=6bf25e4f8631)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** DNA carries a net negative charge from the phosphate of every nucleotide, which is what lets an electric field move its fragments at all
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 4  
  The field pulls DNA towards the anode, and reading the electrode's sign onto the molecule flips the charge to positive; the fragments run to the anode precisely because the backbone is negative.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact that DNA fragments carry a negative charge, demonstrated by options that are only sign alternatives - 'Negatively charged', 'Neutral', 'Positively charged' - with no criterion printed to work from.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q64

## Q66

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Double fertilization is exhibited by

- (1) Algae
- (2) Fungi
- **(3)** Angiosperms
- (4) Gymnosperms

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Double fertilization is a characteristic feature exhibited by angiosperms.
2. It involves syngamy and triple fusion.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Double fertilisation is syngamy (male gamete + egg $\rightarrow$ $2n$ zygote) plus triple fusion (male gamete + two polar nuclei $\rightarrow$ $3n$ primary endosperm nucleus), an event unique to angiosperms, so the answer is option $3$.

## Q67

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

The water potential of pure water is

- (1) Less than zero
- (2) More than zero but less than one
- (3) More than one

**Publisher's solution**

## Q69

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

DNA replication in bacteria occurs

- (1) Within nucleolus
- **(2)** Prior to fission
- (3) Just before transcription
- (4) During S-phase

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. DNA replication in bacteria occurs prior to fission.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

## Q73

Biology · both · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

What is the criterion for DNA fragments movement on agarose gel during gel electrophoresis?

- **(1)** The smaller the fragment size, the farther it moves
- (2) Positively charged fragments move to farther end
- (3) Negatively charged fragments do not move
- (4) The larger the fragment size, the farther it moves

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. During gel electrophoresis, DNA fragments separate (resolve) according to their size through sieving effect provided by agarose gel.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. DNA fragments are negatively charged, so they move towards the anode, and the agarose matrix acts as a sieve; smaller fragments therefore move farther, which is option $1$.

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Hypothalamus secretes GnRH which stimulates anterior pituitary gland for the secretion of gonadotropins (FSH and LH).

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. GnRH from the hypothalamus reaches the anterior pituitary and stimulates release of the gonadotropins LH and FSH (oxytocin is released from the posterior pituitary, FSH/LH are not; relaxin is not a pituitary hormone), so option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Hormonal Regulation of Reproduction · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 13)

![printed question 78](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q078_ba60e3be_29102026.png?v=ba60e3befddb)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 78](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q078_271e8715_29102026.png?v=271e87152d2b)

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** GnRH from the hypothalamus acts on the anterior pituitary, which then secretes the two gonadotropins LH and FSH.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 4  
  The gland is right and LH is right, and the second hormone is filled in with oxytocin because oxytocin is the reproduction hormone most firmly held; one wrong half sinks the whole option.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated line on GnRH's target and effect, demonstrated by option 1's 'Anterior pituitary gland and stimulates secretion of LH and FSH'.
- **Note:** E1's pattern read the long options as `statement_options`; the printed shape is a single-blank completion ('acts on ___') whose options each carry a gland plus a hormone pair, so qtype is recorded as mcq. That compound option is what makes half_right available here.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q79

Biology · zoology · The Living World

Which of the following represents order of 'Horse'?

- **(1)** Perissodactyla
- (2) Caballus
- (3) Ferus
- (4) Equidae

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Horse belongs to order perissodactyla of class mammalia.
2. Perissodactyla includes odd-toed mammals.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In the taxonomic hierarchy of the horse, the order is Perissodactyla, the family is Equidae and caballus/ferus are species-level epithets, so the order is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Order and Class · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

- **Concept:** Taxonomic ranks of the horse: Perissodactyla is its order, Equidae its family and caballus its species name.

## Q80

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

Phosphonol pyruvate (PEP) is the primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in

- **(1)** $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants
- (2) $\mathrm{C_2}$ plants
- (3) $\mathrm{C_3}$ and $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants
- (4) $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. PEP is $3\mathrm{C}$ compound which serves as primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in the mesophyll cell cytoplasm of $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants like maize, sugarcane, Sorghum etc.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants the primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in mesophyll cells is phosphoenol pyruvate (PEP), fixed by PEP carboxylase to oxaloacetic acid; in $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants the primary acceptor is RuBP, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** The C₄ Pathway · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 13)

![printed question 80](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q080_55e40455_29102026.png?v=55e404559440)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** In the C4 pathway PEP in the mesophyll is the primary CO2 acceptor, while in C3 plants CO2 is accepted on RuBP.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 3  
  A C4 leaf does run the Calvin cycle as well, so a student holding 'C4 plants have both pathways' upgrades that to 'PEP serves both kinds of plant', and the option's sound C4 half carries its wrong C3 half.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated acceptor of one named pathway, demonstrated by option 1's '$\mathrm{C_4}$ plants' against option 4's '$\mathrm{C_3}$ plants', where the acceptor is RuBP instead.

## Q81

Biology · botany · Ecosystem

Which ecosystem has the maximum biomass?

- (1) Grassland ecosystem
- (2) Pond ecosystem
- (3) Lake ecosystem
- **(4)** Forest ecosystem

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. High productive ecosystem are
2. – Tropical rain forest
3. – Coral reef
4. – Estuaries
5. – Sugarcane fields

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Of the four options, the forest ecosystem holds the largest standing crop (biomass), because trees store large amounts of organic matter in their wood. Pond and lake ecosystems are dominated by short-lived phytoplankton, and grasslands by herbs, so their biomass is much lower. This gives option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Types of Ecosystems · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

![printed question 81](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q081_074ed012_29102026.png?v=074ed012449a)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 81](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q081_a747a313_29102026.png?v=a747a313bd08)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Comparison** (`compare`) — the difference between two things, or the correct pairing
- **Concept:** Standing biomass piles up where long-lived woody tissue accumulates, so a forest outweighs grassland and freshwater ecosystems.
- **Trap:** **No trap** (`no_trap`) — nothing pulls; the other options are only filler  
  All four options are the ecosystem examples the chapter prints in one sentence -- forest, grassland and desert as terrestrial, pond, lake and wetland as aquatic -- and not one of them is attached to a biomass figure anywhere in that chapter, so no wrong option is reached by a remembered line; two of the four, Pond and Lake, are the same kind of freshwater body and carry the same verdict as each other, and the comparison is settled by the standing crop of wood only the forest holds.
- **Why:** This question requires four named ecosystems to be ranked on one quantity, demonstrated by the stem's 'maximum biomass' with 'Grassland', 'Pond', 'Lake' and 'Forest' as the whole of the options.
- **Note:** Recorded as no_trap rather than forcing a mechanism onto Grassland: the only route offered to a wrong option would be that the chapter draws its pyramid figures on a grassland, and a figure's subject is not a biomass claim. `other_options` is left empty because the finding is that none of the three carries its own route.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q82

Biology · zoology · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

A disease caused by an autosomal primary non-disjunction is

- (1) Klinefelter's syndrome
- (2) Turner's syndrome
- (3) Sickle cell anemia
- **(4)** Down's syndrome

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Down’s syndrome is caused by non-disjunction of $21^{\mathrm{st}}$ chromosome.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Down's syndrome is trisomy of autosome $21$ ($47$ chromosomes) caused by non-disjunction of an autosome; Klinefelter's ($44+XXY$) and Turner's ($44+X0$) are sex-chromosome aneuploidies and sickle cell anemia is a single-base substitution, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Chromosomal Disorders · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

![printed question 82](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q082_74e6cfc3_29102026.png?v=74e6cfc32a74)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 82](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q082_93eeff5c_29102026.png?v=93eeff5c5d38)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Non-disjunction can add or lose an autosome or a sex chromosome: Down's syndrome is trisomy of autosome 21, while Klinefelter's and Turner's are sex-chromosome aneuploidies.
- **Trap:** **Condition dropped** (`condition_missed`) — drop the condition the stem states and the option becomes true — option 1  
  Drop the stem's word 'autosomal' and Klinefelter's answers the question, because it is a trisomy produced by the very non-disjunction the stem names; the chromosome gained there is an X, not an autosome.
- **Why:** This question requires two printed criteria to be met at once, demonstrated by 'autosomal primary non-disjunction', which Klinefelter's and Turner's satisfy only in its second half and 'Sickle cell anemia' only in its first.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q83

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

The vascular cambium normally gives rise to

- (1) Primary phloem
- **(2)** Secondary xylem
- (3) Periderm
- (4) Phelloderm

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. During secondary growth, vascular cambium gives rise to secondary xylem and secondary phloem.
2. Phelloderm is formed by cork cambium.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Vascular cambium forms secondary xylem on its inner side and secondary phloem on its outer side; primary phloem arises from procambium, while periderm and phelloderm arise from cork cambium, so the answer is option $2$ (Secondary xylem).

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

![printed solution 83](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q083_ba63d74b_29102026.png?v=ba63d74bae2c)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** The vascular cambium adds secondary xylem inwards and secondary phloem outwards; primary tissues and the cork-cambium derivatives come from elsewhere.
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 1  
  The remembered rule 'the vascular cambium cuts off xylem inside and phloem outside' lands on the only phloem option and the word 'primary' goes unread, though the primary phloem was laid down by the procambium before any cambium began working.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated product of one named meristem, demonstrated by the stem's 'vascular cambium' set against options printing the cork cambium's derivatives, 'Periderm' and 'Phelloderm'.

## Q84

Biology · botany · Microbes in Human Welfare

Which of the following is correctly matched for the product produced by them?

- (1) Methanobacterium : Lactic acid
- (2) Penicillium notatum : Acetic acid
- **(3)** Saccharomyces cerevisiae : Ethanol
- (4) Acetobacter aceti : Antibiotics

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Saccharomyces cerevisiae is commonly called Brewer’s yeast.
2. It causes fermentation of carbohydrates producing ethanol.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Methanobacterium produces methane (biogas), not lactic acid; Penicillium notatum produces the antibiotic penicillin, not acetic acid; Acetobacter aceti produces acetic acid, not antibiotics; Saccharomyces cerevisiae produces ethanol, so only option $3$ is correctly matched.

**Subtopic:** Chemicals, Enzymes and Other Bioactive Molecules · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

![printed question 84](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q084_06b695b1_29102026.png?v=06b695b1f567)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 84](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q084_966fa226_29102026.png?v=966fa226690a)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Each industrial microbe in the chapter has one printed product: yeast gives ethanol, Acetobacter aceti acetic acid, Penicillium notatum penicillin and Methanobacterium methane.
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 2  
  A student sure of 'Acetobacter aceti makes acetic acid' strikes out option 4, which pairs that bacterium with antibiotics, and then carries the loosened 'acetic acid' to the only other option offering it without asking what Penicillium notatum makes.
- **Why:** This question requires each organism's own printed product, demonstrated by the stem's 'correctly matched for the product produced by them' and option 3's 'Saccharomyces cerevisiae : Ethanol'.
- **Note:** E1's pattern called this `statement_options`; the printed shape is four organism-and-product pairs judged whole, which the rulebook calls pair_options and maps onto qtype statements, so the recorded value stands. `asks` is fact_line and not compare: each option asks what one named microbe produces, and no two things have to be told apart.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q85

Biology · zoology · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

Thalassemia and sickle cell anemia are caused due to a problem in globin molecule synthesis. Select the correct statement.

- (1) Both are due to a quantitative defect in globin chain synthesis
- **(2)** Thalassemia is due to less synthesis of globin molecules
- (3) Sickle cell anemia is due to a quantitative problem of globin molecules
- (4) Both are due to a qualitative defect in globin chain synthesis

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Thalassemia differs from sickle-cell anaemia in that the former is a quantitative problem of synthesising too few globin molecules while the latter is a qualitative problem of synthesising an incorrectly functioning globin.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Thalassemia is a $\text{quantitative}$ defect: too few globin molecules are synthesised, so option $2$ is a correct statement while options $1$, $3$ and $4$ each misclassify at least one disorder (sickle cell anemia is a $\text{qualitative}$ defect, an incorrectly functioning globin from $\text{Glu} \rightarrow \text{Val}$ at position $6$ of the $\beta$ chain); answer is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Thalassemia, Sickle-cell Anaemia · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

![printed question 85](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q085_f16831b6_29102026.png?v=f16831b64454)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 85](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q085_7a3ea92f_29102026.png?v=7a3ea92f9742)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Comparison** (`compare`) — the difference between two things, or the correct pairing
- **Concept:** Thalassemia is a quantitative defect -- too few globin chains made -- while sickle cell anaemia is a qualitative one, a globin chain of the wrong sequence.
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 3  
  A student holding the quantitative-qualitative contrast but not which disorder sits on which side reads the pair the wrong way round, and this option is the exact swap: it hands sickle cell anemia the label thalassemia owns.
- **Why:** This question requires two neighbouring disorders to be held apart on one axis, demonstrated by options that cross 'quantitative' and 'qualitative' with 'Both' and with each disorder singly.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q86

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Flowers which have single ovule in the ovary and are packed into inflorescence are usually pollinated by

- (1) Bee
- **(2)** Wind

- (4) Water

1. Wind pollination or anemophily is favoured by flowers having a single ovule in each ovary, and numerous flowers packed in an inflorescence.
2. Wind pollination is a non-directional pollination.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Wind-pollinated (anemophilous) flowers often have a single ovule in each ovary and numerous flowers packed into an inflorescence (e.g. corn cob), so the answer is Wind, option $2$.

- **Concept:** Wind-pollinated flowers are reduced to a single ovule in each ovary and massed into an inflorescence, the corn cob being the standing example.

## Q87

Biology · zoology · Neural Control and Coordination

Receptor sites for neurotransmitters are present on

- (1) Pre-synaptic membrane
- (2) Tips of axons
- **(3)** Post-synaptic membrane
- (4) Membranes of synaptic vesicles

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Pre-synaptic membrane is involved in the release of neurotransmitter in the chemical synapse.
2. The receptors sites for neurotransmitters are present on post-synaptic membrane.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. At a chemical synapse, neurotransmitter released from the pre-synaptic axon terminal diffuses across the synaptic cleft and binds to specific receptors present on the post-synaptic membrane, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Transmission of Impulses · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

<!--ecat-->

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** At a chemical synapse the transmitter is released from the pre-synaptic terminal and binds receptors that sit only on the post-synaptic membrane.

  The two membranes are the same two words with only the prefix changed, so a student who holds that the answer is a 'synaptic membrane' and not which prefix takes the one printed first; swap the pair for two unlike words and the pull dies, which is the test that makes this likeness rather than position.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated location of the receptors, demonstrated by option 3's 'Post-synaptic membrane' against option 1's 'Pre-synaptic membrane', which differs from it only in its prefix.

## Q88

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

During DNA replication, Okazaki fragments are used to enlongate

- (1) The lagging strand towards replication fork
- (2) The leading strand away from replication fork
- **(3)** The lagging strand away from the replication fork
- (4) The leading strand towards replication fork

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Two DNA polymerase molecules work simultaneous at the DNA fork, one on the leading strand and the other on the lagging strand.
2. Each Okazaki fragment is synthesized by DNA polymerase at lagging strand in $5' \rightarrow 3'$ direction.
3. New Okazaki fragments appear as the replication fork opens further.
4. As the first Okazaki fragment appears away from the replication fork, the direction of elongation would be away from replication fork.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Okazaki fragments are formed only on the lagging strand (template $3' \rightarrow 5'$ relative to fork movement), so options $2$ and $4$ (leading strand) are ruled out.
2. DNA polymerase adds nucleotides only in the $5' \rightarrow 3'$ direction, so on the lagging strand each short fragment is synthesised in the direction opposite to fork movement, i.e. away from the replication fork.
3. Hence Okazaki fragments elongate the lagging strand away from the replication fork, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** The Machinery and the Enzymes of Replication · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 14)

![printed question 88](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q088_081c612b_29102026.png?v=081c612b633b)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 88](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q088_7aee7033_29102026.png?v=7aee7033bdb7)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Rule applied** (`apply_rule`) — a rule you already know, used on a case you have not seen
- **Concept:** DNA polymerase can only add nucleotides 5' to 3', so the lagging strand is built as short Okazaki fragments running away from the advancing fork.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  A student who correctly ties Okazaki fragments to the lagging strand then pictures synthesis chasing the fork, because that is where the template is being opened; the strand half is right and the direction half sinks the option.
- **Why:** This question requires the 5'-to-3' rule applied to the lagging template, demonstrated by options that cross 'lagging' and 'leading' with 'towards' and 'away from the replication fork'.
- **Note:** E1's pattern called this `statement_options`; the options are fragments completing the stem's blank rather than statements to be judged, so qtype is recorded as mcq. `asks` is apply_rule because the direction away from the fork is not a printed line -- it follows from the polymerase's one-way restriction. The stem's 'enlongate' is a misspelling in the record as stored.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q89

Biology · zoology · Digestion and Absorption

Which of the following options best represents the enzyme composition of pancreatic juice?

- (1) Amylase, pepsin, trypsinogen, maltase
- (2) Peptidase, amylase, pepsin, rennin
- **(3)** Lipase, amylase, trypsinogen, procarboxy-peptidase
- (4) Amylase, peptidase, trypsinogen, rennin

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Rennin and Pepsin enzymes are present in the gastric juice.
2. Maltase is present in the intestinal juice.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Pancreatic juice contains $\text{trypsinogen}$, $\text{chymotrypsinogen}$, $\text{procarboxypeptidases}$, $\text{amylase}$, $\text{lipase}$ and $\text{nucleases}$; pepsin and rennin are gastric enzymes, and maltase and peptidases come from the intestinal juice, so only option $3$ (lipase, amylase, trypsinogen, procarboxypeptidase) fits.

**Subtopic:** Digestion of Food · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

![printed question 89](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q089_4d09fa87_29102026.png?v=4d09fa878730)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 89](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q089_98af7a70_29102026.png?v=98af7a70a2ad)

#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Pancreatic juice carries amylase, lipase, nucleases and the inactive proteases trypsinogen, chymotrypsinogen and procarboxypeptidase; pepsin and rennin belong to gastric juice.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  Two of the four names, amylase and trypsinogen, are genuinely on the pancreatic list, so a student who checks the set on the strength of the names recognised accepts it and never tests pepsin, which is gastric, or maltase, which is intestinal.
- **Why:** This question requires the printed composition of one named juice, demonstrated by option 3's 'Lipase, amylase, trypsinogen, procarboxy-peptidase' against sets that mix in 'pepsin' and 'rennin'.

## Q90

Biology · both · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Anaphase promoting complex (APC) is a protein degradation machinery necessary for proper mitosis of animal cells. If APC is defective in a human cell, which of the following is expected to occur?

- (1) Chromosomes will be fragmented
- **(2)** Chromosomes will not segregate
- (3) Recombination of chromosome arms will occur
- (4) Chromosomes will not condense

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Anaphase Promoting Complex (APC) is a protein necessary for separation of daughter chromosomes during anaphase.
2. If APC is defective then the chromosomes will fail to segregate during anaphase.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. APC is needed for the metaphase-to-anaphase transition: it tags securin (and cyclin B) for degradation.
2. Degrading securin releases separase, which cleaves cohesin holding sister chromatids together, so $\text{APC} \rightarrow \text{securin}\downarrow \rightarrow \text{separase active} \rightarrow \text{cohesin cleaved}$.
3. If APC is defective, cohesin is not cleaved, so the sister chromatids are not pulled apart in anaphase: chromosomes will not segregate, which is option $2$.
4. Fragmentation, recombination of arms and failed condensation (prophase) are not caused by loss of APC, so options $1$, $3$ and $4$ are ruled out.

**Subtopic:** Phases of Cell Cycle · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

![printed question 90](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q090_9bc784f3_29102026.png?v=9bc784f3f316)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 90](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q090_a9baa333_29102026.png?v=a9baa333df8a)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Rule applied** (`apply_rule`) — a rule you already know, used on a case you have not seen
- **Concept:** The anaphase-promoting complex acts at the metaphase-to-anaphase step, so losing it leaves the sister chromatids unseparated.
- **Trap:** **Wrong site attacked** (`wrong_site_attacked`) — the reagent is right but it attacks the other site, atom or bond than the one the question turns on — option 4  
  A student reading only 'necessary for proper mitosis' picks a mitotic event that can fail but places the failure at the wrong stage: condensation happens in prophase, while the complex the stem names acts at the entry to anaphase.
- **Why:** This question requires a named complex to be placed at its stage and the consequence read off, demonstrated by the stem's 'Anaphase promoting complex' together with 'If APC is defective in a human cell'.
- **Note:** wrong_site_attacked is used in the §5.1 sense, 'the right change made at the wrong position, stage or site': the option keeps the right kind of failure and moves it from anaphase to prophase. The record's `negative_words_seen` holds NOT, but that NOT is inside the options ('will not segregate'), not in the task.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q91

Biology · zoology · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

The genotypes of a Husband and Wife are $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}}$ and $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$. Among the blood types of their children, how many different genotypes and phenotypes are possible?

- (1) 3 genotypes ; 4 phenotypes
- **(2)** 4 genotypes ; 3 phenotypes
- (3) 4 genotypes ; 4 phenotypes
- (4) 3 genotypes ; 3 phenotypes

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Husband $\times$ Wife: $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}}$ $\times$ $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/figures_agent/q091_sol_1_2624e7b3_29102026.svg?v=2624e7b3b479)

2. Number of genotypes $= 4$
3. Number of phenotypes $= 3$
4. $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{A}}$ and $\mathrm{I^{A}i} = \mathrm{A}$
5. $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}} = \mathrm{AB}$
6. $\mathrm{I^{B}i} = \mathrm{B}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Husband $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}}$ makes gametes $\mathrm{I^{A}}$ and $\mathrm{I^{B}}$; wife $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$ makes gametes $\mathrm{I^{A}}$ and $\mathrm{i}$.
2. Combining gametes gives $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{A}}$, $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$, $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}}$, $\mathrm{I^{B}i}$, i.e. $4$ genotypes.
3. $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{A}}$ and $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$ are blood group A, $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}}$ is AB (codominance), $\mathrm{I^{B}i}$ is B, i.e. $3$ phenotypes.
4. $4$ genotypes and $3$ phenotypes is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Incomplete Dominance & Co-dominance · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

![printed question 91](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q091_cac766de_29102026.png?v=cac766dead2d)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 91](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q091_a0c3c9f0_29102026.png?v=a0c3c9f0410b)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Two answers** (`two_part`) — the answer has two halves, usually joined by 'respectively'
- **Asks:** **Calculation** (`calculate`) — put numbers in and get a number out
- **Concept:** The cross $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{B}} \times \mathrm{I^{A}i}$ gives four genotypes, but $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{A}}$ and $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$ are both blood group A, so only three phenotypes appear.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 3  
  The square really does give four genotypes, so the first half is right; the second half comes from counting one blood group per box, which drops the dominance of $\mathrm{I^{A}}$ over $i$ that makes $\mathrm{I^{A}I^{A}}$ and $\mathrm{I^{A}i}$ the same group A.
- **Why:** This question requires both counts to be taken from the cross, demonstrated by the stem's 'how many different genotypes and phenotypes are possible' with each option printing the two numbers together.
- **Note:** E1's pattern called this `statement_options`; the stem asks for two counts and every option prints both, so the printed shape is two_part.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q92

Biology · botany · Biological Classification

Viroids differ from viruses in having

- (1) DNA molecules without protein coat
- (2) RNA molecules with protein coat
- **(3)** RNA molecules without protein coat
- (4) DNA molecules with protein coat

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Viroids are sub-viral agents as infectious RNA particles, without protein coat.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Viroids consist of free $\text{RNA}$ without a protein coat, whereas viruses have their nucleic acid enclosed in a protein coat (capsid), so the difference is RNA molecules without protein coat, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Viroids, Prions & Lichens · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** A viroid is a free RNA of low molecular weight with no protein coat, which is what sets it apart from a virus.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  The headline difference, no protein coat, is remembered and that half of the option is right; the genome half is filled in as DNA because DNA is the genetic material carried by default.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated composition of a viroid, demonstrated by the stem's 'differ from viruses in having' with options crossing DNA against RNA and coat against no coat.

## Q93

Biology · botany · Organisms and Populations

Mycorrhizae are the example of

- (1) Amensalism
- (2) Antibiosis
- **(3)** Mutualism
- (4) Fungistasis

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Mycorrhizae is a symbiotic association of fungi with roots of higher plants.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Mycorrhiza is a symbiotic association of a fungus with the roots of higher plants; the fungus helps absorb phosphorus from the soil and the plant provides the fungus with energy-yielding carbohydrates, so both partners benefit ($+/+$), which is mutualism, option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Mutualism · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

![printed question 93](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q093_64df0014_29102026.png?v=64df0014e14c)

![printed solution 93](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q093_1a1566a4_29102026.png?v=1a1566a483c4)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Mycorrhiza is the standing example of mutualism: the fungus takes sugars from the plant and the plant takes minerals through the fungus.
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 1  
  Amensalism is the only other interaction name from the chapter's own table of interspecific interactions, so a student who recalls mycorrhiza as an example in that section but not which row it sits on takes the neighbouring row; swap the two names for unlike words and the pull survives, because the printed table and not any resemblance is carrying the student.
- **Why:** This question requires the interaction mycorrhiza is filed under, demonstrated by option 3's 'Mutualism' against 'Amensalism', the only other interaction the chapter's list carries.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q94

The morphological nature of the edible part of coconut is

1. Coconut has double endosperm with liquid endosperm and cellular endosperm.

  In the seeds dissected first -- pea, gram, groundnut -- the fleshy edible part is the cotyledon, and that food store is printed in the same passage as the endosperm, so a student carrying 'the edible part of a seed is the cotyledon' applies it to the coconut; swap the two names for unlike words and the pull survives, so position rather than likeness is doing the work.

## Q95

Biology · zoology · Neural Control and Coordination

Myelin sheath is produced by

- (1) Astrocytes and Schwann Cells
- (2) Oligodendrocytes and Osteoclasts
- (3) Osteoclasts and Astrocytes
- **(4)** Schwann Cells and Oligodendrocytes

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Oligodendrocytes are neuroglial cells which produce myelin sheath in central nervous system while Schwann cell produces myelin sheath in peripheral nervous system.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Myelin is laid down by Schwann cells around peripheral axons and by oligodendrocytes around central axons, while astrocytes and osteoclasts make no myelin, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Neuron as Structural and Functional Unit of Neural System and Types of Axon · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

![printed question 95](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q095_bbc95149_29102026.png?v=bbc9514928c0)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Myelin is laid down by Schwann cells around peripheral axons and by oligodendrocytes around axons inside the central nervous system.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  Schwann cells is the name tied to myelin in the peripheral nerve and this option carries it, so the pair is taken on the strength of its first half; astrocytes support and nourish neurons and wrap no axon in myelin.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact of which cells lay down myelin, demonstrated by the stem 'Myelin sheath is produced by' and four options that each bundle two named cell types with no criterion printed to work from.
- **Note:** Each option is a pair of cell names accepted or rejected whole, the codebook's pair_options; the qtype axis carries no pair value and two_part is for a stem that asks two different things, so the shape is recorded as mcq and stated here.

## Q96

Biology · zoology · Reproductive Health

Match the following sexually transmitted diseases (Column - I) with their causative agent (Column - II) and select the correct option.

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Gonorrhea | (i) | HIV |
| (b) | Syphilis | (ii) | Neisseria |
| (c) | Genital Warts | (iii) | Treponema |
| (d) | AIDS | (iv) | Human Papilloma virus |

- (1) (a) (iii), (b) (iv), (c) (i), (d) (ii)
- (2) (a) (iv), (b) (ii), (c) (iii), (d) (i)
- (3) (a) (iv), (b) (iii), (c) (ii), (d) (i)
- **(4)** (a) (ii), (b) (iii), (c) (iv), (d) (i)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Gonorrhoea – Neisseria (Bacteria)
2. Syphilis – Treponema (Bacteria)
3. Genital Warts – Human papilloma virus (Virus)
4. AIDS – HIV (Virus)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Gonorrhea is caused by $Neisseria$ $gonorrhoeae$, so $(a) \rightarrow (ii)$.
2. Syphilis is caused by $Treponema$ $pallidum$, so $(b) \rightarrow (iii)$.
3. Genital warts are caused by Human Papilloma virus, so $(c) \rightarrow (iv)$.
4. AIDS is caused by HIV, so $(d) \rightarrow (i)$.
5. The match $(a)(ii), (b)(iii), (c)(iv), (d)(i)$ is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Sexually Transmitted Infections (STIs) · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 15)

![printed question 96](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q096_52039532_29102026.png?v=52039532c2e5)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 96](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q096_b42a4c2b_29102026.png?v=b42a4c2b0274)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Match the columns** (`match_columns`) — two labelled lists to pair, the answer is a pairing code
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Each sexually transmitted infection NCERT names has one causative agent: Neisseria for gonorrhoea, Treponema for syphilis, Human Papilloma virus for genital warts, HIV for AIDS
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 3  
  Syphilis-(iii) Treponema and AIDS-(i) HIV are the two pairings this code already gets right, so a student who confirms those two and stops never tests (a) and (c), where Neisseria and Human Papilloma virus have been exchanged.
- **Why:** This question requires the causative agent NCERT states for each named disease, demonstrated by the stem's instruction to match 'sexually transmitted diseases (Column - I) with their causative agent (Column - II)'.

## Q97

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

Which among these is the correct combination of aquatic mammals?

- (1) Dolphins, Seals, Trygon
- **(2)** Whales, Dolphins Seals
- (3) Trygon, Whales, Seals
- (4) Seals, Dolphins, Sharks

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Sharks and Trygon (sting ray) are the members of chondrichthyes (cartilaginous fish) while whale, Dolphin and Seals are aquatic mammals belong to class mammalia.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Trygon (stingray) and sharks are cartilaginous fishes (Chondrichthyes), not mammals, so options $1$, $3$ and $4$ each contain a non-mammal.
2. Whales, dolphins and seals are all aquatic mammals (Mammalia), so option $2$ is the correct combination.

**Subtopic:** Class – Amphibia, Reptilia, Aves and Mammalia · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 16)

![printed question 97](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q097_127a7b45_29102026.png?v=127a7b45f2ca)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Whales, dolphins and seals are mammals that happen to live in water, while Trygon and the sharks are cartilaginous fishes, so a shared habitat is not evidence of a shared class.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  Dolphins and seals are genuine aquatic mammals, so two thirds of the set checks out, and Trygon rides in on the stem's word 'aquatic'; it is the sting ray, printed among the cartilaginous fishes, not among the mammals.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated class of each named animal, demonstrated by the stem asking for 'the correct combination of aquatic mammals' while every option bundles three names that must all belong.
- **Note:** Options 1 and 3 carry the identical defect, two genuine aquatic mammals plus Trygon, and neither dominates, so the trap is recorded on option 1 and option 3 carries the same route under other_options. Each option is a set of three names judged whole, the codebook's pair_options; recorded as mcq because the qtype axis has no value for a bundled set.

## Q98

Biology · botany · Morphology of Flowering Plants

Coconut fruit is a

- (1) Berry
- (2) Nut
- (3) Capsule
- **(4)** Drupe

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Coconut fruit is a drupe.
2. A drupe develops from monocarpellary superior ovary and are one seeded.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Coconut is a one-seeded fruit from a monocarpellary superior ovary whose pericarp is split into a thin epicarp, a fibrous mesocarp and a hard stony endocarp, which defines a drupe (option $4$).

**Subtopic:** The Fruit · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** A drupe is a one-seeded fruit whose endocarp is stony, which is exactly what the coconut's hard shell is; the everyday name 'nut' is not its botanical fruit type.

  The fruit's own name carries the word 'nut' and kitchen usage calls it one, so the option answers what a coconut is called rather than which botanical fruit type it is.

## Q99

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

A dioecious flowering plant prevents both:

- **(1)** Autogamy and geitonogamy
- (2) Geitonogamy and xenogamy
- (3) Cleistogamy and xenogamy
- (4) Autogamy and xenogamy

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. When unisexual male and female flowers are present on different plants the condition is called dioecious and it prevents both autogamy and geitonogamy.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A dioecious plant bears only male or only female flowers, so the male and female flowers are on separate plants. It therefore prevents both autogamy (pollen to the same flower) and geitonogamy (pollen to another flower of the same plant), and only xenogamy can occur. This is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Outbreeding Devices · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 16)

![printed question 99](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q099_e1afac7d_29102026.png?v=e1afac7de7f6)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 99](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q099_2cfce30e_29102026.png?v=2cfce30e6a74)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Rule applied** (`apply_rule`) — a rule you already know, used on a case you have not seen
- **Concept:** In a dioecious plant the male and female flowers sit on different individuals, so no pollen can reach a stigma on the same plant: autogamy and geitonogamy are both shut out and only xenogamy is left.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 4  
  Autogamy is certainly blocked once the sexes are on separate plants, and geitonogamy looks safe to anyone who reads 'pollen from another flower' as crossing, so xenogamy is taken as the second mode prevented, which is the one mode dioecy actually guarantees.
- **Why:** This question requires the definition of dioecy applied to each named pollination mode, demonstrated by the stem 'prevents both' forcing two of autogamy, geitonogamy, cleistogamy and xenogamy to be tested against flowers borne on different plants.
- **Note:** The one member of this group not answered from a single stated name: the options pair modes, and deciding means testing the condition 'different plants' against each term's definition, so asks is apply_rule rather than fact_line. Options are pairs judged whole, the codebook's pair_options, recorded as mcq as elsewhere in this group.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q100

Biology · botany · Biological Classification

Which of the following are found in extreme saline conditions?

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

## Q102

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

With reference to factors affecting the rate of photosynthesis, which of the following statements is not correct?

- (1) Increasing atmospheric $\mathrm{CO_2}$ concentration upto $0.05\%$ can enhance $\mathrm{CO_2}$ fixation rate
- **(2)** $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants responds to higher temperatures with enhanced photosynthesis while $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants have much lower temperature optimum
- (3) Tomato is a greenhouse crop which can be grown in $\mathrm{CO_2}$ - enriched atmosphere for higher yield
- (4) Light saturation for $\mathrm{CO_2}$ fixation occurs at $10\%$ of full sunlight

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants photosynthesis is decreased at higher temperature due to increased photorespiration.
2. $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants have higher temperature optimum because of the presence of pyruvate phosphate dikinase enzyme, which is sensitive to low temperature.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Options $1$, $3$ and $4$ are correct: raising $\mathrm{CO_2}$ to about $0.05\%$ increases fixation, tomato is grown in $\mathrm{CO_2}$-enriched greenhouses for higher yield, and light saturation occurs at about $10\%$ of full sunlight. Option $2$ is wrong because $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants, not $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants, respond to higher temperatures with more photosynthesis, while $\mathrm{C_3}$ plants have the lower temperature optimum. So the answer is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Factors Affecting Photosynthesis · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 16)

![printed question 102](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q102_77184c28_29102026.png?v=77184c28e31d)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 102](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q102_128a8073_29102026.png?v=128a80735bcb)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Among the factors affecting photosynthesis, the temperature optimum is higher in C4 plants than in C3 plants
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 4  
  Light saturation at one tenth of full sunlight reads far too low to a student picturing crops in open sun, so the figure looks like the one the paper altered. NCERT prints exactly this line, so the statement is correct and the stem asked for the one that is not.
- **Why:** This question requires four printed claims about the factors affecting photosynthesis to be checked against the lines NCERT states, demonstrated by the stem 'which of the following statements is not correct?' and by option 2 inverting the optima — '$\mathrm{C_3}$ plants responds to higher temperatures with enhanced photosynthesis while $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants have much lower temperature optimum'.
- **Note:** E1 gave statement_options; recorded as qtype statements because the stem itself asks which of the printed 'statements is not correct', with no lettered block above the options. The three wrong options are all lines NCERT prints, so the question's single device is its polarity.

## Q103

Biology · botany · Plant Kingdom

Life cycle of Ectocarpus and Fucus respectively are

- (1) Diplontic, Haplodiplontic
- **(2)** Haplodiplontic, Diplontic
- (3) Haplodiplontic, Haplontic
- (4) Haplontic, Diplontic

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Ectocarpus has haplodiplontic life cycle and Fucus has diplontic life cycle.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Ectocarpus has both a multicellular haploid gametophyte and a multicellular diploid sporophyte, so its life cycle is $\text{haplodiplontic}$; Fucus has a diploid plant body with only the gametes haploid, so its life cycle is $\text{diplontic}$, which matches option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Phaeophyceae · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

- **Concept:** Among brown algae the life cycles differ: Ectocarpus alternates haploid and diploid generations (haplodiplontic) while Fucus is diplontic
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 1  
  Option 1 holds the right two life-cycle names with the two algae swapped, so a student who remembers that this pair supplies the haplodiplontic and the diplontic example but not which alga is which hands Ectocarpus the diplontic label.
- **Why:** This question requires the life-cycle type NCERT prints for each of two named brown algae, demonstrated by the stem 'Life cycle of Ectocarpus and Fucus respectively are' and by every option pairing two of the three printed patterns.

## Q104

Biology · botany · Biodiversity and Conservation

The region of Biosphere Reserve which is legally protected and where no human activity is allowed is known as

- (1) Buffer zone
- (2) Transition zone
- (3) Restoration zone
- **(4)** Core zone

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Biosphere reserve is protected area with multipurpose activities.
2. It has three zones
3. (a) Core zone – without any human interference
4. (b) Buffer zone – with limited human activity
5. (c) Transition zone – human settlement, grazing cultivation etc., are allowed.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

## Q105

A gene whose expression helps to identify transformed cell is known as

1. In recombinant DNA technology, selectable markers helps in identifying and eliminating non-transformants and selectively permitting the growth of the transformants.

## Q106

Biology · both · Cell: The Unit of Life

Which of the following components provides sticky character to the bacterial cell?

- (1) Nuclear membrane
- (2) Plasma membrane
- **(3)** Glycocalyx
- (4) Cell wall

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Sticky character of the bacterial wall is due to glycocalyx or slime layer.
2. This layer is rich in glycoproteins.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The bacterial cell envelope has three layers: the outermost $\text{glycocalyx}$, then the $\text{cell wall}$, then the $\text{plasma membrane}$. The $\text{glycocalyx}$ (slime layer or capsule) is the one that gives the cell its sticky character, which is option $3$.

## Q108

Fruit and leaf drop at early stages can be prevented by the application of

- (1) Ethylene

- (3) Gibberellic acid
- (4) Cytokinins

## Q109

Biology · botany · Environmental Issues

Which one of the following statements is not valid for aerosols?

- (1) They alter rainfall and monsoon patterns
- **(2)** They cause increased agricultural productivity
- (3) They have negative impact on agricultural land
- (4) They are harmful to human health

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Aerosols can cause various problems to agriculture through its direct or indirect effects on plants.
2. However continually increasing air pollution may represent a persistent and largely irreversible threat to agriculture in the future.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Aerosols (fine particulate pollutants) alter rainfall and monsoon patterns, harm agricultural land/crops and are harmful to human health, so options $1$, $3$ and $4$ are valid; increased agricultural productivity is not an effect of aerosols, so the invalid statement is option $2$.

The label is condition_missed because each remaining option is a true printed effect, so only dropping the stem's 'not valid' makes any of them answerable.

## Q110

Biology · both · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Which of the following options gives the correct sequence of events during mitosis?

- **(1)** Condensation $\rightarrow$ nuclear membrane disassembly $\rightarrow$ arrangement at equator $\rightarrow$ centromere division $\rightarrow$ segregation $\rightarrow$ telophase
- (2) Condensation $\rightarrow$ crossing over $\rightarrow$ nuclear membrane disassembly $\rightarrow$ segregation $\rightarrow$ telophase
- (3) Condensation $\rightarrow$ arrangement at equator $\rightarrow$ centromere division $\rightarrow$ segregation $\rightarrow$ telophase
- (4) Condensation $\rightarrow$ nuclear membrane disassembly $\rightarrow$ crossing over $\rightarrow$ segregation $\rightarrow$ telophase

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The correct sequence of events during mitosis would be as follows
2. (i) Condensation of DNA so that chromosomes become visible occurs during early to mid-prophase.
3. (ii) Nuclear membrane disassembly begins at late prophase or transition to metaphase.
4. (iii) Arrangement of chromosomes at equator occurs during metaphase, called congression.
5. (iv) Centromere division or splitting occurs during anaphase forming daughter chromosomes.
6. (v) Segregation also occurs during anaphase as daughter chromosomes separate and move to opposite poles.
7. (vi) Telophase leads to formation of two daughter nuclei.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Crossing over occurs only in meiosis (pachytene of prophase I), not mitosis, so options $2$ and $4$ are eliminated.
2. In mitotic prophase chromatin condenses into chromosomes, and by the end of prophase the nuclear envelope disassembles; option $3$ omits nuclear membrane disassembly, so it is incomplete.
3. The correct order is condensation $\rightarrow$ nuclear membrane disassembly $\rightarrow$ arrangement at equator (metaphase) $\rightarrow$ centromere division $\rightarrow$ segregation (anaphase) $\rightarrow$ telophase, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Prophase and Metaphase · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 17)

![printed question 110](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q110_20dba70b_29102026.png?v=20dba70bef24)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 110](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q110_3be969b5_29102026.png?v=3be969b52a31)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Sequence** (`order`) — a sequence to put in the right order, or a trend to arrange
- **Concept:** Mitosis runs condensation, nuclear membrane disassembly, alignment at the equator, centromere division, segregation, telophase - and no crossing over occurs anywhere in it.
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 3  
  Every event this option prints is in its right place, so a check that only tests whether each event follows the one before finds nothing wrong and never notices that nuclear membrane disassembly has been left out.
- **Why:** This question requires the order of mitotic events, demonstrated by the stem 'the correct sequence of events during mitosis' and four chains built from the same events. half_right is the label because option 3's defect is an omitted step inside an otherwise correct order, which the rulebook defines as a true but incomplete set.
- **Note:** E1 read this as arrange_in_order; the printed shape is four whole chains chosen as one answer, which maps to qtype mcq, with the sequence demand recorded in asks.

## Q112

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

If there are 999 bases in an RNA that codes for a protein with 333 amino acids, and the base at position 901 is deleted such that the length of the RNA becomes 998 bases, how many codons will be altered?

- (1) 11
- **(2)** 33
- (3) 333
- (4) 1

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. If deletion occurs at $901^{\mathrm{st}}$ position the remaining $98$ bases specifying for $33$ codons of amino acids will be altered.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $999$ bases code for $\frac{999}{3} = 333$ codons, codon $n$ covering bases $3n-2$ to $3n$.
2. Base $901 = 3(301) - 2$, so it is the first base of codon $301$.
3. Bases $1$ to $900$ (codons $1$ to $300$) are unaffected by the deletion.
4. Deleting base $901$ causes a frameshift, altering every codon from $301$ to $333$.
5. Number altered $= 333 - 301 + 1 = 33$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Effect of Mutation on Genetic Code · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 18)

![printed question 112](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/crops/q112_214c4579_29102026.png?v=214c45795e3f)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 112](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/29102026/neet_papers/2017/2017_B/solutions/q112_51d7017e_29102026.png?v=51d7017e352e)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Calculation** (`calculate`) — put numbers in and get a number out
- **Concept:** A single base deletion shifts the reading frame only from its own codon onward, so the codons altered are those from the deletion point to the end of the message.
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 3  
  'A frameshift ruins the whole protein' reaches for the total codon count, and 333 is the number the stem has already supplied for the amino acids - it answers how many codons there are, not how many change.
- **Why:** This question requires the count of codons lying downstream of a stated deletion point, demonstrated by the stem fixing the deletion at 'position 901' and asking 'how many codons will be altered'. answers_other_question is the label because 333 is a true count the stem itself prints - the codons in the message - and it answers a different question from the one asked.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q113

Biology · zoology · Human Health and Disease

Transplantation of tissues/organs fails often due to non-acceptance by the patient's body. Which type of immune-response is responsible for such rejections?

- **(1)** Cell-mediated immune response
- (2) Hormonal immune response
- (3) Physiological immune response
- (4) Autoimmune response

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Non-acceptance or rejection of graft or transplanted tissues/organs is due to cell mediated immune response.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Graft rejection happens because the body tells 'self' from 'non-self' tissue, and T-lymphocytes attack the foreign graft, so it is the cell-mediated immune response, which is option $1$.

- **Concept:** Graft rejection is the cell-mediated arm of acquired immunity at work - the recognition of non-self by T cells, not antibody action and not autoimmunity.
